Directions: In this section, you are going to read a passage with ten statements attached to it. Each statement

                              Can playing video games help you get a better job?

A) The gaming industry is larger than films and music combined, yet few of us are likely to list our
   achievements in playing computer games as work experience in our résumé. But why not? Businesses are
    waking up to the skills gamers can bring to the workplace.

B) One start-up is convinced that the skills learned by playing games — hard-won through years of training and
    battle — can be applied to real-life work situations. And Game Academy reckons its belief is backed up by
    hard data. Do you enjoy unusual puzzle games like Portal, or tower defence games like Defense Grid? The



                               懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/paper/2025-12-2/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 6 -->

   team has found that IT workers play those more than average. But if you prefer Civilization, Total War, or
   X-Com, where strategy and resource management are key, then you might have more in common with
   managers.

C) Game Academy's idea is simple: analyse gamers' habits from their online gaming profile, and offer courses in
   valuable skills that reflect their aptitudes — skills they can practise and refine in-game. And there is already
   a growing acceptance that gaming skills are transferable.

D) Even the military is hiring gamers. "The ability to absorb information, react swiftly and coordinate actions
   whilst remaining calm under pressure are often attributes of people that are good at gaming," according to a
   Royal Air Force spokesperson. Those skills are part of what the Royal Air Force is looking for "in a variety
   of roles". "Skills acquired through gaming can be very relevant to certain areas."

E) "There are plenty of soft skills that gamers can utilise in a professional setting, such as teamwork, problem
   solving and strategic planning," says Ryan Gardner, a regional director with Hays recruitment. But does that
   really mean your top 100 ranking in Overwatch should go down in your résumé? "It's about how you either
   make it relevant to the job you are applying for, or how it makes you more interesting as a potential
   employee," Mr Gardner says.

F) Two years ago, a Glasgow University study made headlines for suggesting gaming could make students more
   successful. But "the research hasn't really changed minds, at least not yet," says one of the authors of the
   study, Dr Matthew Barr. He's now writing a book on the topic. "For now, I think the negative reputation
   around games means that telling someone you're a determined gamer is more likely to harm your chances of
   getting a job. But if someone can explain how they've led a team in an online game, for example, they may
   be able to persuade an employer that this is useful," says Dr Barr.

G) Playing games casually is unlikely to help your career prospects. But part of Game Academy's suggestion is
   to transform casual gamers into "conscious" ones, applying critical thinking to developing their skills. "We
   see gameplay as a resource of talent," says co-founder David Barrie. "Why can't we put in our résumé our
   gaming victories and achievements?" he asks. "If they say they want leadership — why can't I point to my
   years of leading World of Warcraft raids?"

H) There are already plenty of gamers out there who know that their hobby has made them who they are.
   Earlier this year, Matthew Ricci told gaming site Kotaku: "If you're playing EVE Online you basically
   already have an MBA." Often made fun of in gaming circles as a "spreadsheet simulator(                   电子表格模拟 )",
   the economy of the fictional EVE universe is driven by real market principles. If you want to build a new
   spaceship, the raw material has to be mined by another player. Manufacturing costs come into effect, and
   commodities fluctuate in price based on demand and the distance to haul the commodity.

I) Mr Ricci, who had always dreamed of being the boss of his own company, ran an in-game corporation
   comprising hundreds of players. Eventually, he realised he could transfer his skills to real-life business —
  instead of doing it for free. He restructured Zentech, once a taxation vehicle for his father's business, and it is
  now in its fourth year helping international brands enter the Canadian market. EVE teaches skills like
  creativity, leadership, organisation, and conflict resolution, he maintains. He credits his success to his family,
  his strong desire to run his own business — and "a damn good company in Iceland that made a damn good
  game".

J) Digital entrepreneur and business consultant Mia Bennett says: "In more traditional settings, gaming is still
   imagined to be the pursuit of teenage boys — a waste of time." But there are some links to skills like

   "decision-making, the ability to anticipate and future planning," she says. "It also helps with meta-skills —

K) Twelve years into his career, Mark Long, a radiotherapy physicist with the NHS in Surrey, doesn't get as
   much gaming time as he used to. "I would love to say that my gaming skills allow me to destroy cancer
   using linear accelerators like photon(    光子 ) blasters, but that's not quite the reality." Instead, he credits old-
   school games like Palace of Magic with exposing him to computers. Every new gaming upgrade improved

   translates. "When creating treatment plans, the aim is to optimise the radiation dose to the tumour and
   restrict it as much as possible to healthy surrounding tissues and organs," he says. It's about "repeating the
   process, but each time doing something slightly different to improve the result". Not unlike achieving a high
   score or a perfect run. "Most videogames are essentially puzzles to solve," he says. "And problem-solving is
   a big part of my job."

L) "Over at Game Academy, Mr Barrie is aware they still have a mountain to climb. The employer community
   will need the science," he says, but confesses he only became a true believer when he started asking players
   about their biggest in-game achievements. "The scale and scope of their answers were amazing," he says.
   "But ask them the same about their work, and they don't know." "The engagement and powerful skills that
   people get playing a game — why can't work be like that?" he asks.

    Junk food is now a staple of many Americans' diets. Advertising campaigns from the snack food companies,
often featuring sports stars, send the message that we can neutralize any negative effects of consuming their
products simply by getting more physical exercise. But recent studies show a lack of exercise is not to blame for
rising obesity rates. Bad diets are.

                  干预
     Interventions(     ) to help reduce junk food consumption are especially important for children and
teenagers. Prevention is better than cure because obesity is so difficult to treat. Unfortunately, while health
education has shown some success among young children, teens have been harder to reach.

    Now a large-scale study has tried an innovative approach to change teenagers' attitudes towards healthy
eating, and the results are promising. The researchers argued that previous interventions have probably been
unsuccessful because of a major flaw: they focused on a future, healthier you and assumed that this would be
enough motivation for teenagers. In contrast, the new intervention cleverly exploits teenagers' instinct for
rebelliousness and autonomy, and the value they place on social justice.

    To do this, researchers had students read an article on the food industry. It revealed a secret about the
manipulative and deceptive strategies used to make junk food more addictive and characterize the products as
healthy. The article also explained how advertising campaigns specifically target very young and poor people,
causing harm for these vulnerable groups. Afterwards, the participants read a fictional survey of teens who
wanted to "fight back against the companies by buying and eating less processed food"

    After the intervention, participants associated healthy eating with autonomy and social justice. The teenagers
also rated healthy eating as being more appealing. Importantly, there were also some promising effects of the
new intervention on actual behavior. A day later the students were offered a choice of snacks and drinks in a
seemingly unrelated context. The teens chose healthy snacks and drinks (such as fruit or water) more often over
unhealthy options (like biscuits and soda). Apparently associating a healthy diet with teenagers' own values seems
to be a promising avenue to prevent obesity.

Passage Two
Questions 51 to 55 are based on the following passage.

     Adults dream during REM (rapid eye movement) sleep and infants have loads of REM. So, it might be fair
to assume that babies have tons of dreams.

   But scientists believe REM serves a completely different purpose for newborns and infants than dreaming.
When babies are in REM, it allows their brain to develop pathways, connections, and eventually, learn languages.

     Since your baby doesn't dream at this stage of brain development, it's safe to assume babies do not have
nightmares, either. Nightmares stem from exposure to trauma(                 创伤
                                                                 ), an overactive imagination, and the normal
stresses of everyday life.

     Are you wondering, what do babies dream about? Good question, but the answer is nothing! So, when do
babies start dreaming? The general consensus is that they start dreaming around the age of two. Psychologist
David Foulkes studies children (from very small kids to teens) to bring the secrets of their dreams to the light of
day. In his lab, he lets kids fall asleep and then wakes them 3 times a night and asks them to describe what
they recall.

     Foulkes' findings are unsurprising. Basically, little kids have little dreams. But exactly what kids see while
dreaming depends on their age. As children develop and grow, their dreams do too.

     Dreams of very small kids are usually just snapshots(               快照 ), looking much more like a slideshow than a
movie, when compared to the dreams of adults. They heavily feature animals and other familiar sights, like
images of people eating. According to Foulkes, "Children's dream life…seems to be similar to their waking
imagination and narration." Kids ages 5 – 9 begin seeing moving images and characters in action. Dreams now
include multiple events strung together, one after the other. They also start developing greater ability to
remember dreams. Still, that's not always the case: When roused during REM sleep, 25% of the kids in Foulkes'
studies had no recollection of dreaming, a trend that continues through age 9.

     So, if your baby seems to be in a stressful state while sleeping or is upset upon waking, there may be other
factors at play.

真题卷第2套(2025-12) • 英语四级
Part I


Directions: Suppose it is proposed that all university students should be required to learn academic
writing. You are now to write a response by stating what you think of the proposal. You will have 30
minutes to write the essay. You should write at least 120 words but no more than 180 words.

【参考范文 · 156 词】 写作无唯一标准答案，以下为达标示范

It has recently been proposed that all university students should be required to learn academic writing. In my
view, this proposal deserves our full support, for such a skill is far more than an optional refinement.

For one thing, academic writing trains students to organize ideas logically and cite sources honestly. When I
wrote my first term paper, I struggled to structure arguments and nearly copied online material by mistake; a
writing course would have taught me both clear reasoning and academic integrity. For another, this ability pays
off well beyond campus. Whether a graduate applies for a research position or drafts a report at work, the
capacity to express thoughts precisely on paper often determines success.

Admittedly, mastering academic writing takes time and effort. Nevertheless, the long-term rewards clearly
outweigh the cost. Therefore, I strongly believe universities should make this course compulsory, ideally
starting from the first year so that students benefit throughout their studies.

【参考译文】

近来有人提议，所有大学生都应必修学术写作。在我看来，这一提议值得我们全力支持，因为这项技能远
不只是锦上添花。

一方面，学术写作训练学生把思路组织得有条理，并如实引用资料来源。我第一次写学期论文时，就苦于
难以搭建论证结构，还险些误抄了网上的材料；一门写作课本可以同时教会我清晰的逻辑与学术诚信。另
一方面，这项能力在校园之外同样受用。无论毕业生是申请科研岗位，还是在职场撰写报告，把想法在纸
面上精准表达的能力，往往决定着成败。

诚然，掌握学术写作需要时间和努力。然而，其长远回报显然远大于付出的成本。因此，我坚信大学应将
这门课程设为必修，最好从大一就开设，让学生在整个学业期间都从中受益。



                          懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/2025-12-2/part1/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 12 -->

【第 1 段】复述加表态。首段落实『复述加表态开篇』：It has recently been proposed that... 用同义
改写复述提案、点题；紧接 In my view, this proposal deserves our full support 一句亮明支持立场，
兑现 stating what you think of the proposal，开篇即拿任务分。

【第 2 段】分点加例证。第二段落实『分点理由配例证』：For one thing... for another... 两条理由并
列；中间 When I wrote my first term paper... 用亲身场景把『论文规范与学术诚信』坐实，Whether a
graduate... 把价值延伸到就业深造，两条都咬住学术写作本身，不空谈。

【第 3 段】让步加重申。第三段落实『让步转折收尾』：Admittedly... 先认成本，Nevertheless... 转折
抬高回报，Therefore, I strongly believe... 顺势重申应必修并补『从大一开设』的温和建议，立场首尾
一致、毫不摇摆。


ANALYSIS · 审题与立意


这道题到底要写什么、立意是什么
动笔前先钉死本题的核心写作任务与读者语域 ，再定中心立意与要展开的要点 —— 这些
是本题特有的内容判断，不是通用套路。




  核心任务

  对提案亮明态度
  本题的核心任务是：针对 it is proposed that all university students should be required to learn
  academic writing 这条提案，写一篇回应并 stating what you think of the proposal。一句话钉
  死——不是介绍学术写作是什么、也不是罗列它的好坏，而是必须明确说出你赞成还是反对
  （或有条件支持）。四级近年这类「就某提案表态」的命题很常见，任务分全压在「有没有站
  队」上。




  读者 · 语域

  面向阅卷人的正式议论
  本题是纯议论文，没有具体收信人。写作要求只说 write a response，没有指定身份或对象，因
  此把读者默认为阅卷人即可：用正式书面语，不用 I wanna、gonna 这类口语缩略，也不用写
  称呼和落款。语域定成「客观、克制、有据的议论腔」，通过 In my view、Therefore 这类衔接
  词体现态度层次即可。




                  懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/2025-12-2/part1/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 13 -->

  中心立意

  全力支持必修学术写作
  本篇立场：坚定支持 all university students should be required to learn academic writing。中
  心论点一句话——学术写作是每个大学生都该掌握的核心学术能力，理应设为必修。立场鲜
  明、不骑墙，正好回应任务要求的 stating what you think of the proposal。



  要展开的要点


   1. 打牢论文规范与学术诚信

    理由一扣住学术写作的本职价值：它教学生把观点组织得有逻辑、并如实引用他人成果，直接
    关系到论文规范与学术诚信。这条能用『第一次写学期论文时结构混乱、险些误抄』的亲身场
    景撑起一整段，具体可感。

   2. 为深造与就业铺路

    理由二把价值延伸到校园之外：无论毕业后申请科研岗位还是在职场写报告，精准的书面表达
    往往决定成败。这一条紧扣『学术写作』迁移出的长远回报，避免写成『英语写作』或空泛的
    『写作有好处』。

   3. 让步后重申立场

    第三点作收束：承认掌握学术写作确实费时费力，但长远回报远大于成本，因此仍应必修，并
    温和补一句『最好从大一开始开设』作为补充建议。



STRUCTURE · 谋篇与逐段


怎么分段、每段写什么、能套哪些句架
按写作家族给出现成的段落框架（每段承担什么功能），并为每段配可直接套用的 功能
句架（留出填本题内容的空位）。段序与上方参考范文一一对应。




  第 1 段 复述提案＋亮立场
          第一段：先一句话复述 it is proposed that all university students should be required to
          learn academic writing 这条提案，再立刻给出明确态度——支持，并埋下一句总起，引
          出后面的理由。



  第 2 段 两条理由＋例证
          第二段是论证主体：用 For one thing / For another 分点摆两条理由（论文规范与诚信、
          深造与就业），每条各配一个具体场景或后果，把理由说实。




                  懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/2025-12-2/part1/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 14 -->

第 3 段 让步转折＋重申建议
       第三段收尾：先让步承认掌握学术写作费时费力，再转折强调回报更大，最后重申应必
       修并补一句温和建议（从大一开设）。




第 1 段 点题并宣布支持
落到本题：复述『有人提议所有大学生都应必修学术写作』，随即表态支持，理由是这项技
能远不止是锦上添花，回应了 stating what you think of the proposal 的核心要求。


 同义改写复述提案 ·              It has recently been proposed that ___                     （把写作要求里的提案换个
 说法引出，避免逐字照抄）。
 一句话亮明立场 ·            In my view, this proposal deserves ___                     （填 our full support / careful
 reconsideration   等，态度词决定站哪边）。


第 2 段 诚信规范＋职业回报
本段两条理由：其一，学术写作训练逻辑组织与如实引用，用『初写学期论文结构混乱、险
些误抄』的场景坐实论文规范与学术诚信；其二，这项能力校园外同样管用，无论申请科研
岗还是职场写报告，精准表达常决定成败。


 分点罗列理由 ·          For one thing, ___; for another, ___                    （两条并列理由的骨架，各填一
 条）。
 亲身场景举例 ·          When I ___, I struggled to ___                  （用一个自己的经历把抽象理由变具
 体）。

 把价值延伸到未来 ·              Whether a graduate ___ or ___, the capacity to ___ often
 determines success    （把好处推到就业/深造场景）。




                   懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/2025-12-2/part1/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 15 -->

  第 3 段 认成本后重申必修
  本段承认掌握学术写作确需时间和努力，随即强调长远回报远大于成本，因此重申大学应将
  其设为必修，并补一句『最好从大一开设，让学生全程受益』的温和建议。


    让步认成本 ·     Admittedly, ___ takes time and effort                  （先退一步，承认代价或顾虑）。
    转折压过顾虑 ·     Nevertheless, the ___ clearly outweigh the ___                          （用转折把回报抬到成
    本之上）。

    顺势重申立场 ·     Therefore, I strongly believe ___                 （收束回立场，把态度钉死并可带一句
    建议）。

REVIEW · 自检避坑


本题在四个评分维度怎样算达标、容易在哪丢分
按阅卷四维（内容切题 / 结构连贯 / 语言准确 / 字数格式）给出本题达标要点，并把最容
易踩的低分写法摆出来 + 给改法 —— 这是写作版的「狠拆诱人错项」。




   内容切题    必须真站队
           本题达标线是全文有清晰立场，回应 stating what you think of the proposal；最易失
           分是只描述提案、只罗列利弊却不表态，那样即使语言好也丢核心任务分。



   结构连贯    三段各司其职
           本题按首段亮立场、主体段两条理由、尾段让步重申来分段，靠 For one thing / For
           another / Admittedly / Therefore 显出层次；忌一段到底或立场前后摇摆。



   语言准确    态度词要用准
           本题靠 In my view、Admittedly、Nevertheless、Therefore 这些表态与让步衔接词撑
           起层次，要用对位置；同时注意 cite sources、academic integrity 等词的拼写与搭
           配，别把 academic writing 写成泛泛的 English writing。




                 懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/2025-12-2/part1/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 16 -->

  字数格式        卡死上下限
              本题硬性要求 You should write at least 120 words but no more than 180 words，低
              于下限或超过上限都扣分；三段紧凑议论正好落进区间，别为凑字重复啰嗦。



典型低分写法 · 反面示例与改法



 只析利弊不表态

 反面示例：Some people think academic writing is useful, while others think it is a waste of time.
 There are advantages and disadvantages.（通篇分析利弊、始终不说自己怎么想，丢掉任务分。）
 改法：把它换成一句明确表态——In my view, academic writing should be a required course for all
 university students.（先站队，再展开理由。）



 空谈写作好处

 反面示例：Writing is very important. It can improve our life and make us successful.（空泛到换成
 任何技能都成立，且滑成了泛写作。）改法：Academic writing teaches students to build
 arguments logically and cite sources honestly, which safeguards academic integrity.（具体扣住学
 术写作的本职价值。）



 首尾自相矛盾

 反面示例：首段写 I strongly support this proposal，尾段却写 After all, maybe it is not necessary
 to make it compulsory.（立场摇摆，直接失分。）改法：尾段保持同向——Therefore, I firmly
 believe this course should be made compulsory.（让步认成本后仍坚持原立场。）




                    懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/2025-12-2/part1/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

PAPER & KEY · 听力题和解析


听力题和解析
本卷全部 25 题的标准答案与完整录音文字稿由懒笔记基于2025年12月·第2套 英语四级听
力原卷逐题核对整理。与试卷排版同一卷面层级：Part/Section 标题与各节 Directions 照
原卷排印——真实听力考卷只印选项，题干由录音念出。其下按录音篇目逐篇排印：每篇
先给完整录音文字稿（逐段中译），再逐题给出卷面题目与该题解析（题目、翻译、答
案、定位、信号、替换与排除）。



真题卷第2套(2025-12) • 英语四级
以下按录音篇目排印：每篇先给录音原文与逐段中译（考卷上没有，考试时录音只播一遍、卷面只印选项），再逐
题给出卷面题目与解析。

Part II                                Listening Comprehension                                                     (25 minutes)


Section A

Directions: In this section, you will hear three news reports. At the end of each news report, you will
hear two or three questions. Both the news report and the questions will be spoken only once. After you
hear a question, you must choose the best answer from the four choices marked A), B), C) and D). Then
mark the corresponding letter on Answer Sheet 1 with a single line through the centre.

Questions 1 and 2 are based on the news report you have just heard.

1   Customs and Border Protection officials are seeing a rise in people illegally bringing eggs from
Mexico into border states, such as Texas.
美国海关与边境保护局官员发现,从墨西哥非法携带鸡蛋进入得克萨斯州等边境州的人数正在增加。

2   The U.S. average price for a dozen large eggs last month hit $4.25. That's more than double their
$1.78 price in December, 2023. Meanwhile, 30 eggs in Mexico sell for just $3.4.
上个月美国一打大号鸡蛋的平均价格达到4.25美元。这是2023年12月1.78美元价格的两倍多。与此同时,
墨西哥30个鸡蛋的售价仅为3.4美元。

3   Those bringing eggs into the U.S. risk fines of up to $10,000. But officials say they usually seize and
destroy the eggs. They also impose a $300 penalty. The Department of Agriculture banned eggs from
Mexico in 2012. This was due to the risk of bird flu. Officials said most people arriving at international




                        懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/listening/2025-12-2/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 19 -->

borders with eggs are truthful about their purchases. They simply don't know bringing eggs is
not allowed.
将鸡蛋带入美国的人面临最高1万美元的罚款。但官员表示,他们通常会没收并销毁鸡蛋。他们还会处以
300美元的罚金。农业部于2012年禁止从墨西哥进口鸡蛋。这是由于禽流感的风险。官员表示,大多数携
带鸡蛋抵达国际边境的人对其购买行为如实申报。他们只是不知道携带鸡蛋是不被允许的。

4   "Generally, the items are declared during the first inspection. Then the person can abandon the eggs
without consequence," a Customs and Border Protection spokesperson said.
美国海关与边境保护局发言人表示:"通常情况下,这些物品在首次检查时就会申报。然后当事人可以放弃这
些鸡蛋而不会产生后果。"

5   Even so, there have been a very small number of cases recently when authorities discovered eggs
during further inspections.
即便如此,最近仍有极少数情况是当局在进一步检查时发现了鸡蛋。

1. A) It has gone up sharply.
    B) It has been fluctuating.
    C) It has risen because of bird flu.
    D) It has been consumers' concern.

【题目】 What do we learn from the news report about the egg price in the U.S.?

【翻译】 从新闻报道中我们了解到美国鸡蛋价格的什么情况？
            A) 价格大幅上涨。                B) 价格一直在波动。                          C) 价格因禽流感而上涨。

            D) 价格一直是消费者关注的问题。

【答案】 A （细节题） It has gone up sharply.

【定位】 The U.S. average price for a dozen large eggs last month hit $4.25. That's more than double
            their $1.78 price in December, 2023.
            上个月美国一打大号鸡蛋的平均价格达到了 4.25 美元，这比 2023 年 12 月的 1.78 美元还
            高出一倍多。

【信号】 数字对比+more than double。答案句的信号是数字加比较：hit $4.25 与 more than
            double their $1.78 price——从 1.78 涨到 4.25、还『翻了不止一倍』，是明确的大幅上
            涨。新闻里出现两个价格数字相比较，往往就是答案句。

【替换】 more than double their $1.78 price ↔ gone up sharply
            倍数=大幅




                         懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/listening/2025-12-2/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 20 -->

【排除】 B) 未提及

         It has been fluctuating. 说价格『来回波动』，但录音只给了从 1.78 到 4.25 的单向上
         涨，没有任何下跌或反复，方向不符。
         C) 原词陷阱

         It has risen because of bird flu. 把上涨原因归给禽流感。录音确实念到 bird flu，但那是
         解释 The Department of Agriculture banned eggs from Mexico（为何禁止从墨西哥带
         蛋）的原因，不是涨价的原因——盗用了原词却接错了因果。
             借用原文：This was due to the risk of bird flu.
         D) 未提及

         It has been consumers' concern. 说蛋价成了消费者的担忧。录音通篇没提消费者的态度
         或担忧，只客观报了价格数字，属无中生有。


2. A) They don't want to abandon their eggs.
  B) They don't know they will be heavily fined.
  C) They don't know they are forbidden to bring eggs.
  D) They don't declare their eggs during the inspection.

【题目】 What does the news report say about most people arriving at American borders
         with eggs?

【翻译】 新闻报道关于携带鸡蛋抵达美国边境的大多数人说了什么？
         A) 他们不想放弃自己的鸡蛋。                             B) 他们不知道会被处以重罚。

         C) 他们不知道禁止携带鸡蛋。                             D) 他们在检查时没有申报自己的鸡蛋。

【答案】 C （细节题） They don't know they are forbidden to bring eggs.

【定位】 Officials said most people arriving at international borders with eggs are truthful about their
         purchases. They simply don't know bringing eggs is not allowed.
         官员说，多数带着鸡蛋到达国际边境的人对自己买的东西是如实交代的。他们只是不知道带
         鸡蛋是不被允许的。

【信号】 题干同词组+紧跟解释句。答案句信号是题干原词组在录音里复现：most people arriving
         at international borders with eggs 几乎照搬了题干，它后面紧跟的 They simply don't
         know bringing eggs is not allowed 就是对这群人的正面交代，正是答案落点。

【替换】 They simply don't know bringing eggs is not allowed ↔
         They don't know they are forbidden to bring eggs.
         not allowed=forbidden




                      懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/listening/2025-12-2/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 21 -->

【排除】 A) 原词陷阱

           They don't want to abandon their eggs. 说他们不愿放弃鸡蛋。录音里确有 abandon
           the eggs 这个词，但原文是说『在第一次检查时申报、然后可以无后果地放弃鸡蛋』，根
           本没说他们『不愿放弃』——盗用 abandon 一词，意思却恰相反。
               借用原文：Then the person can abandon the eggs without consequence
           B) 偷换概念

           They don't know they will be heavily fined. 说他们不知会被重罚。录音里 risk fines of
           up to $10,000、a $300 penalty 确实提到罚款，但原文说这群人不知道的是『带蛋被禁
           止』，不是『不知会被罚款』——把『不知禁令』偷换成了『不知罚款』。
               借用原文：Those bringing eggs into the U.S. risk fines of up to $10,000.
           D) 与原文相反

           They don't declare their eggs during the inspection. 说他们检查时不申报。录音里
           declared during the first inspection 确实有 declare 和 inspection，但原文是说『物品
           一般会在第一次检查时被申报』，即多数人是申报的，与选项的『不申报』正好相反。
               借用原文：Generally, the items are declared during the first inspection.
Questions 3 and 4 are based on the news report you have just heard.

1   Nettie's House, a restaurant in America, has been hit with criticism after announcing on the website
that children under the age of 10 are no longer welcome.
美国一家名为Nettie's House的餐厅在其网站上宣布不再欢迎10岁以下儿童后遭到批评。

2   Among the reasons given for the decision, the restaurant cited "noise levels, lack of space for high
chairs, cleaning up crazy messes, and the liability of kids running around the restaurant". They decided
that it was time to take control. The decision wasn't made lightly, but some recent events pushed them to
implement this new policy.
餐厅给出的决定理由包括"噪音水平、高脚椅空间不足、清理混乱不堪的场面以及孩子在餐厅跑来跑去的
责任问题"。他们决定是时候采取管控措施了。这个决定并非轻率做出,但最近发生的一些事件促使他们实
施这项新政策。

3   Responding to the post, one person commented that "It is messed up beyond another level. Children
are children. Mums are tired and don't have time to cook. Not welcoming children under 10 is a
complete slap in the face to families." He felt so disappointed in the establishment.
针对这篇帖子,有人评论说"这简直乱得不成样子。孩子就是孩子。妈妈们很累,没有时间做饭。不欢迎10岁
以下儿童完全是对家庭的一记耳光。"他对这家餐厅感到非常失望。

4   There are others, however, who showed support for the restaurant's decision. They said it has the right
to implement any rules it wants.
然而,也有其他人对餐厅的决定表示支持。他们说餐厅有权实施任何它想要的规定。




                        懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/listening/2025-12-2/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 22 -->

3. A) It is no longer critical of younger customers.
  B) It has banned kids under 10 from dining there.
  C) It is no longer as noisy and crowded as before.
  D) It has implemented a decision to expand business.

【题目】 What do we learn from the news report about the restaurant Nettie's House?

【翻译】 从新闻报道中我们了解到关于Nettie's House餐厅的什么信息?
          A) 它不再对年轻顾客持批评态度。                                  B) 它已禁止10岁以下儿童在那里就餐。

          C) 它不再像以前那样嘈杂和拥挤。                                  D) 它已实施了一项扩大业务的决定。

【答案】 B （细节题） It has banned kids under 10 from dining there.

【定位】 Nettie's House, a restaurant in America, has been hit with criticism after announcing on the
          website that children under the age of 10 are no longer welcome.
          美国一家名叫 Nettie's House 的餐厅在网站上宣布不再欢迎 10 岁以下儿童后，遭到了
          批评。

【信号】 新闻首句点题，机构名后紧跟核心事件。短篇新闻第一句通常就把核心事件说清。这里店名
          之后紧跟 announcing on the website that children under the age of 10 are no longer
          welcome，这正是"该餐厅做了什么"的答案句，顺着开头第一句就能抓到。

【替换】 children under the age of 10 are no longer welcome ↔ banned kids under 10 from dining there
          "不再欢迎"="禁止用餐"的同义改写

【排除】 A) 与原文相反

          It is no longer critical of younger customers. 借了原文"针对低龄顾客"这层意思，却说餐
          厅"不再挑剔年轻顾客"——恰恰相反，餐厅是收紧了对儿童的限制；而且 no longer
          critical 的是公众对餐厅的批评，不是餐厅对顾客的态度，方向反了。
              借用原文：children under the age of 10 are no longer welcome
          C) 原词陷阱

          It is no longer as noisy and crowded as before. 抓住了原文提到的 noise levels（吵闹）
          来勾人，但原文只是把吵闹列为出台新规的"理由"，从没说餐厅现在已经变得不吵不挤了。
          听到 noise 就选 C 就中了原词陷阱。
              借用原文：noise levels
          D) 原词陷阱

          It has implemented a decision to expand business. 蹭了原文里 implement this new
          policy 的 implement 一词，但原文实施的是"谢绝儿童"的新政策，根本没提扩张生意，
          expand business 是无中生有。
              借用原文：implement this new policy




                       懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/listening/2025-12-2/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 23 -->

4. A) It is fully aware of frequent customers' feelings.
    B) It caters to the tastes of different customers.
    C) It is entitled to adopt any rules it wants.
    D) It should announce its decisions online.

【题目】 What do supporters say in response to the restaurant's new policy?

【翻译】 支持者对餐厅的新政策作何回应?
            A) 它充分了解常客的感受。                          B) 它迎合不同顾客的口味。

            C) 它有权采用任何它想要的规则。                                  D) 它应该在网上公布其决定。

【答案】 C （态度观点题） It is entitled to adopt any rules it wants.

【定位】 There are others, however, who showed support for the restaurant's decision. They said it has
            the right to implement any rules it wants.
            不过也有另一些人对餐厅的决定表示支持。他们说餐厅有权实施它想要的任何规定。

【信号】 转折 however 引出支持方，应答句紧随。第4段以 There are others, however, who
            showed support 用转折词 however 把镜头从前文反对者切到支持者，紧接着 They said
            it has the right to implement any rules it wants 就是支持者的原话——题问
            supporters，落点正是这句应答。

【替换】 it has the right to implement any rules it wants ↔ It is entitled to adopt any rules it wants
            has the right=is entitled，implement=adopt

【排除】 A) 未提及

            It is fully aware of frequent customers' feelings. 说餐厅"充分了解常客的感受"，但支持
            者原话只讲"餐厅有权定规矩"，从没提常客（frequent customers）也没说店家了解谁的感
            受，这层意思原文整个没出现。
            B) 原词陷阱

            It caters to the tastes of different customers. 蹭了全篇反复出现的 customers 一词，但
            支持者说的是"店家有权定规矩"，不是说它"迎合不同顾客的口味"——caters to tastes 在
            原文没有依据，是借熟词编的好听话。
                借用原文：Children are children
            D) 原词陷阱

            It should announce its decisions online. 蹭了原文 announcing on the website 的"网上
            宣布"，但那只是叙述餐厅当初怎么发的公告，支持者根本没说"店家应该在网上公布决定"，
            且 should 是建议口吻、与本题问的支持表态不符。
                借用原文：announcing on the website
Questions 5 to 7 are based on the news report you have just heard.

1   Security officers were searching a suburb in Australia where an adult snake was believed to be




                         懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/listening/2025-12-2/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 24 -->

wandering after the discovery of a huge, "freshly shed" snake skin.
在澳大利亚一个郊区发现了一张巨大的"新脱落"蛇皮后,安全官员正在搜寻该地区,据信有一条成年蛇在
游荡。

2   The skin was found at a construction site, and the government wrote to residents to warn them. They
were told to observe and, if possible, photograph the animal. If it was moving, watch where it went. It
was recommended not to make contact with the animal.
这张蛇皮是在一处建筑工地发现的,政府向居民发出书面警告。他们被告知要观察这条蛇,如有可能对其拍
照。如果它在移动,要观察它去了哪里。建议不要与该动物接触。

3   The type of snake was determined to be one of the world's largest snake species. It is not poisonous,
but can still attack and kill its victims. This type of snake is classified as a threat to humans, especially
small children, as well as pets. They may carry viruses that are fatal to native snakes, which do not
have resistance.

经确定,这种蛇是世界上最大的蛇类物种之一。它没有毒性,但仍然可以攻击并杀死猎物。这种蛇被归类为
对人类构成威胁,特别是对幼儿以及宠物。它们可能携带对本地蛇类致命的病毒,而本地蛇类对这些病毒没
有抵抗力。

4   If officers found the snake, they would employ a licensed snake catcher to catch it and transport it to
a specialist animal doctor, the government said. If caught, the snake would be examined to determine
where it came from, how long it had been in the area, what it had been eating, whether it was carrying
any diseases of concern and whether it had produced babies.
政府表示,如果官员找到这条蛇,他们将雇用有执照的捕蛇人将其捕获并运送到专业动物医生处。如果被捕
获,将对这条蛇进行检查,以确定它来自哪里、在该地区待了多长时间、吃了什么、是否携带任何令人担忧
的疾病以及是否产下了幼蛇。

5. A) A missing pet.
    B) A grown-up snake.
    C) A huge animal skin.
    D) A snake specialist.

【题目】 What does the report say security officers were searching for?

【翻译】 报道称安全人员在搜寻什么？
           A) 一只走失的宠物。                   B) 一条成年蛇。                    C) 一张巨大的动物皮。                            D) 一位蛇类专家。

【答案】 B （细节题） A grown-up snake.

【定位】 Security officers were searching a suburb in Australia where an adult snake was believed to
           be wandering after the discovery of a huge, "freshly shed" snake skin.
           安保人员正在搜查澳大利亚的一处郊区，据信有一条成年蛇在那里游荡，此前那里发现了一
           张巨大的、刚蜕下的蛇皮。




                        懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/listening/2025-12-2/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 25 -->

【信号】 搜寻主语句直接点对象。答案句是新闻第一句，把『谁在搜+搜什么』一并说清：an adult
          snake was believed to be wandering（一条成年蛇据信在游荡），这才是被搜寻的活物；
          蛇皮 snake skin 只是引出搜寻的线索，不是搜寻目标。

【替换】 an adult snake ↔ A grown-up snake.
          adult=grown-up 同义改写

【排除】 C) 原词陷阱
          选项盗用了录音里真实出现的 a huge, "freshly shed" snake skin（巨大的刚蜕的蛇皮），
          听到 huge 和 skin 很想选 A huge animal skin.。但蛇皮只是引发搜寻的线索，搜寻的对象
          是活蛇本身，故 C 答非所问。
             借用原文：a huge, "freshly shed" snake skin
          A) 未提及

          录音通篇说的是一条野生大蛇，从未提到这是谁走失的宠物。A missing pet. 在原文中没有
          依据，属凭常识臆测（『蛇会不会是谁养的跑了』），录音没说就不能选。
          D) 张冠李戴

          A snake specialist.（捕蛇/治蛇专家）盗用了录音后文 a licensed snake catcher 与 a
          specialist animal doctor 的概念，但这些人是抓到蛇之后才会请的，不是此刻搜寻的对
          象。把『搜寻蛇』偷换成『搜寻专家』，张冠李戴。
             借用原文：a specialist animal doctor
6. A) In a government office.
  B) In a deserted house.
  C) At a local zoo.
  D) At a construction site.

【题目】 Where does the report say the discovery was made?

【翻译】 报道称在哪里有了这一发现？
          A) 在一个政府办公室。                     B) 在一座废弃的房子里。                              C) 在当地一家动物园。

          D) 在一处建筑工地。

【答案】 D （细节题） At a construction site.

【定位】 The skin was found at a construction site, and the government wrote to residents to
          warn them.
          那张蛇皮是在一处建筑工地发现的，政府随后致信居民提醒他们。

【信号】 地点状语 at 直接给地点。答案句里 The skin was found at a construction site 的 at a
          construction site 是典型的地点状语，正面回答了『发现在哪』。问 where 就抓这种 at/in
          + 场所的结构。

【替换】 found at a construction site ↔ At a construction site.
          地点词原样对应




                       懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/listening/2025-12-2/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 26 -->

【排除】 A) 原词陷阱

         选项盗用了同句里的 the government 一词，听到 government 就想选 In a government
         office.。但录音里政府是『写信提醒居民』的施动方，并非发现蛇皮的地点，二者无关，属
         答非所问。
             借用原文：the government wrote to residents
         B) 未提及

         In a deserted house.（废弃房屋）录音从未提到，可能是考生凭『蛇出没=破旧荒地』的
         常识脑补出来的。原文只说工地，没说废屋，无依据。
         C) 未提及

         At a local zoo.（本地动物园）也是常识联想（『蛇=动物=动物园』），录音里完全没有出
         现 zoo。原文发现地是工地，不是动物园，排除。


7. A) Figuring out where it came from.
  B) Curing it of any possible diseases.
  C) Protecting its babies against harm.
  D) Determining how to catch it safely.

【题目】 What did the government say would be done if security officers found what they were
         searching for?

【翻译】 政府表示如果安全人员找到了他们搜寻的目标，将会采取什么措施？
         A) 查明它来自哪里。                   B) 治疗它可能患有的疾病。                                C) 保护它的幼崽免受伤害。

         D) 确定如何安全地捕捉它。

【答案】 A （细节题） Figuring out where it came from.

【定位】 If caught, the snake would be examined to determine where it came from, how long it had
         been in the area, what it had been eating, whether it was carrying any diseases of concern and
         whether it had produced babies.
         一旦抓获，这条蛇将接受检查，以确定它从哪里来、在该区域待了多久、一直在吃什么、是
         否携带值得关注的疾病，以及是否已经产下幼蛇。

【信号】 If caught 引出后续措施。答案句以 If caught（一旦抓获）开头，正对题干的 if…found 假
         设条件，后面 would be examined to determine where it came from 列出抓到后要做的
         事，是本题答案落点。

【替换】 examined to determine where it came from ↔ Figuring out where it came from.
         determine=figure out 同义




                      懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/listening/2025-12-2/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 27 -->

【排除】 B) 偷换概念

           选项盗用了录音 whether it was carrying any diseases of concern 里的 diseases 一词，
           听到『病』就想选 Curing it of any possible diseases.。但原文只是『检查它是否携带疾
           病』，并非『治好它的病』，把『检查』偷换成『治疗』，故错。
              借用原文：whether it was carrying any diseases of concern
           C) 偷换概念

           选项盗用了 whether it had produced babies 里的 babies，让人误选 Protecting its
           babies against harm.。但原文是『查清它是否产过幼蛇』，根本不是『保护幼蛇不受伤
           害』，方向完全不同，属偷换概念。
              借用原文：whether it had produced babies
           D) 原词陷阱

           Determining how to catch it safely. 盗用了 catch（抓）。录音里抓蛇是 employ a
           licensed snake catcher to catch it（雇持证捕蛇人去抓），是直接安排人去抓，并不是
           『研究怎样安全地抓』，故 D 答非所问。
              借用原文：employ a licensed snake catcher to catch it
Section B

Directions: In this section, you will hear two long conversations. At the end of each conversation, you
will hear four questions. Both the conversation and the questions will be spoken only once. After you
hear a question, you must choose the best answer from the four choices marked A), B), C) and D). Then
mark the corresponding letter on Answer Sheet 1 with a single line through the centre.

Questions 8 to 11 are based on the conversation you have just heard.

1   W: Poor Hannah!
W: 可怜的Hannah!

2   M: Why? What happened?
M: 怎么了?发生什么事了?

3   W: Didn't you hear? She won a TV competition and became a millionaire overnight.
W: 你没听说吗?她赢了一个电视竞赛,一夜之间成了百万富翁。

4   M: No way. That's incredible. Are you kidding?
M: 不会吧。太不可思议了。你在开玩笑吗?

5   W: No, I'm serious. I swear it. She went on a TV show where they quiz on general knowledge, and
she won first prize.
W: 没有,我是认真的。我发誓。她参加了一个电视节目,那种考常识问答的,然后她赢得了头奖。

6   M: Wow. That's so lucky of her. How much did she win?
M: 哇。她太幸运了。她赢了多少钱?




                       懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/listening/2025-12-2/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 28 -->

7    W: I'm not sure of the exact figure, but it's a few million Euros, maybe five million Euros after tax.
W: 我不确定具体数字,但有几百万欧元,税后大概五百万欧元。

8    M: I can't believe it. You're really not joking, are you?
M: 我真不敢相信。你真的不是在开玩笑,对吧?

9    W: Honestly, look at my face. Our friend Hannah won a ton of money.
W: 真的,你看我的表情。咱们的朋友Hannah赢了一大笔钱。

10    M: Unbelievable. Then why were you saying "poor Hannah"?
M: 难以置信。那你为什么说"可怜的Hannah"?

11    W: Well, it turns out that she's spent it all already.
W: 嗯,结果她已经把钱全花光了。

12    M: Five million Euros? How is that even possible?
M: 五百万欧元?这怎么可能?

13    W: She bought a house for herself and her husband, a house for her parents, and then a house
for each of her four brothers and sisters. And then she bought her husband a ridiculously expensive
sports car.
W: 她给自己和丈夫买了一套房子,给她父母买了一套房子,然后又给她四个兄弟姐妹每人买了一套房子。之
后她又给她丈夫买了一辆贵得离谱的跑车。

14    M: Wow. Are they all happy? Because that's what matters, isn't it?
M: 哇。他们都开心吗?因为这才是最重要的,不是吗?

15    W: I know for a fact Hannah isn't. You see, she was planning to quit the job she hates and retire
early. But now that she ran out of money, she can't. Basically, she miscalculated how fast she was
burning through the money, and she's now full of regret.
W: 我确定Hannah不开心。你看,她本来计划辞掉她讨厌的工作,提前退休。但现在她钱花光了,做不到了。
基本上,她错误估计了花钱的速度,现在她满心懊悔。

16    M: Why don't they sell that flashy new car?
M: 他们为什么不把那辆炫酷的新车卖掉?

17    W: She wants to, but her husband refuses to get rid of it. He says the car is the best thing that has
ever happened to him, and if she sells it, he will divorce her.
W: 她想卖,但她丈夫拒绝放弃那辆车。他说那辆车是他这辈子遇到的最好的东西,如果她把它卖了,他就跟她
离婚。

8. A) The man doesn't agree Hannah is poor.
     B) The man doesn't believe what she says.
     C) The man doesn't think she can be a millionaire.
     D) The man doesn't consider her to be trustworthy.

【题目】 Why does the woman say she swears it?




                          懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/listening/2025-12-2/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 29 -->

【翻译】 为什么女人说她发誓?
         A) 男人不同意Hannah很穷。                          B) 男人不相信她说的话。

         C) 男人不认为她能成为百万富翁。                                  D) 男人不认为她值得信任。

【答案】 B （推断题） The man doesn't believe what she says.

【定位】 M: I can't believe it. You're really not joking, are you?

         男：我真不敢相信。你真的不是在开玩笑吧？

【信号】 发誓是对男士『不信』的回应。女士反复发誓的触发点，是男士这句 I can't believe it.
         You're really not joking, are you?——男士先表态不信、追问是否开玩笑，女士才被迫发
         誓。这句男士的怀疑就是答案信号，前面 No way. That's incredible. Are you kidding? 也
         铺垫了同样的不信。

【替换】 I can't believe it. ↔ doesn't believe what she says
         I can't believe=doesn't believe

【排除】 A) 原词陷阱

         选项 The man doesn't agree Hannah is poor. 借了录音开头 Poor Hannah! 的 poor 来勾
         人，但男士从没就『Hannah 穷不穷』表过态，他不信的是『中了大奖』这件事，与穷不穷
         无关。
             借用原文：Poor Hannah!
         C) 偷换概念

         选项 The man doesn't think she can be a millionaire. 抓了录音 became a millionaire
         overnight 里的 millionaire，但男士震惊的是『这事是不是真的』，并非质疑『她有没有能
         力成为富翁』——把『不信事实』偷换成了『不信她的能力』。
             借用原文：became a millionaire overnight
         D) 过度推断

         选项 The man doesn't consider her to be trustworthy. 听着和『不信』近似，但
         trustworthy 指『这个人不可信、人品有问题』，男士只是惊讶于消息太离奇、问对方是否
         开玩笑，并没有否定女士的人品，属过度引申。


9. A) She no longer has any money left.
  B) She has been betrayed by her family.
  C) She can no longer hold on to her job.
  D) She has been robbed of 5 million Euros.

【题目】 What do we learn about Hannah from the conversation?

【翻译】 从对话中我们了解到关于Hannah的什么情况?
         A) 她已经没有任何钱了。                        B) 她被家人背叛了。                         C) 她不能再保住她的工作了。

         D) 她被抢劫了500万欧元。

【答案】 A （细节题） She no longer has any money left.




                      懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/listening/2025-12-2/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 30 -->

【定位】 W: Well, it turns out that she's spent it all already.

          女：嗯，原来她已经把钱全花光了。

【信号】 转折后交代真实结局。前文一直在讲 Hannah 中了大奖、好运连连，直到女士这句 it turns
          out that she's spent it all already 才点出真相——钱花光了。it turns out(原来)是语气转
          折信号，转折之后才是这道题要的事实。she ran out of money 再次确认了这一点。

【替换】 she's spent it all already ↔ no longer has any money left
          全花光=不再有钱

【排除】 B) 未提及

          选项 She has been betrayed by her family. 在录音里找不到任何依据——对话提到她给家
          人买房，是付出而非被背叛，『被家人背叛』纯属编造。
          C) 与原文相反

          选项 She can no longer hold on to her job. 抓了录音 quit the job she hates 里的 job，
          但录音是说她原想辞掉讨厌的工作却辞不成、只能继续干，恰恰是『保得住/必须保住』工
          作，选项却说成『保不住』，把意思反了。
                借用原文：she was planning to quit the job she hates and retire early. But now that she ran out of
                money, she can't
          D) 原词陷阱

          选项 She has been robbed of 5 million Euros. 用了录音里真念到的钱数 maybe five
          million Euros after tax 来制造熟悉感，但钱是她自己花光的(买房买车)，不是被人抢走，
          robbed 完全没有依据。
                借用原文：maybe five million Euros after tax
10. A) Fortune.
   B) Family.
   C) Harmony.
   D) Happiness.

【题目】 What does the man think really matters to people?

【翻译】 男人认为对人们来说真正重要的是什么?
          A) 财富。         B) 家庭。            C) 和睦。             D) 幸福。

【答案】 D （细节题） Happiness.

【定位】 M: Wow. Are they all happy? Because that's what matters, isn't it?

          男：哇，他们都开心吗？因为那才是最要紧的，不是吗？

【信号】 matters 原词点名 + 反问强调。男士直接说 that's what matters, isn't it?，其中 matters
          与题干 really matters 对得上；而 that 指的正是前半句的 Are they all happy?——所以他
          认为最要紧的是『开心/幸福』。反问句 isn't it 是加重语气的强调信号。




                         懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/listening/2025-12-2/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 31 -->

【替换】 Are they all happy? ↔ Happiness.
          happy→Happiness 形转名

【排除】 A) 原词陷阱

          选项 Fortune. 和整段『中大奖、五百万』的话题最贴，最易顺手误选，但通篇没有任何人
          说『财富才是最要紧的』，男士点名的是开心，不是钱。
              借用原文：maybe five million Euros after tax
          B) 原词陷阱

          选项 Family. 借了录音里反复出现的家人(给 her parents、四个兄弟姐妹买房)，但男士说
          『要紧』时指的是大家开不开心，不是『家庭』这个概念本身。
              借用原文：a house for her parents, and then a house for each of her four brothers and sisters
          C) 未提及

          选项 Harmony.(和睦)看似和『家人开心』沾点边，但录音里既没念到 harmony 这个词，
          也没人表达『和睦才最重要』，纯属凭话题臆造。


11. A) Divorce her.
   B) Leave home.
   C) Buy her a more expensive model.
   D) Let the best thing happen to them.

【题目】 What does Hannah's husband say he'll do if she sells the new car?

【翻译】 如果她卖掉新车,Hannah的丈夫说他会做什么?
          A) 和她离婚。           B) 离开家。                C) 给她买一辆更贵的车。

          D) 让最好的事情发生在他们身上。

【答案】 A （细节题） Divorce her.

【定位】 W: She wants to, but her husband refuses to get rid of it. He says the car is the best thing that
          has ever happened to him, and if she sells it, he will divorce her.
          女：她想卖，但她丈夫拒绝处理掉它。他说这辆车是他这辈子遇到的最好的东西，如果她卖
          了，他就跟她离婚。

【信号】 if…he will… 条件句直给答案。答案句就是 if she sells it, he will divorce her——题干的
          条件 if she sells 在这里原样出现，紧跟的主句 he will divorce her 就是丈夫扬言要做的
          事。if 引导的条件主句是这道题的答案信号。

【替换】 he will divorce her ↔ Divorce her.
          原句动作直接照搬




                       懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/listening/2025-12-2/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 32 -->

【排除】 B) 偷换概念

           选项 Leave home. 听着和『离婚』情绪相近，但录音里丈夫说的是 divorce(离婚)，从没提
           『离家出走』，属凭联想替换。
           C) 原词陷阱

           选项 Buy her a more expensive model. 抓了录音 a ridiculously expensive sports car
           的 expensive，但录音里丈夫是『拒绝卖掉现有的车』，根本不是要再买更贵的，方向相
           反。
               借用原文：a ridiculously expensive sports car
           D) 原词陷阱

           选项 Let the best thing happen to them. 改自录音 the car is the best thing that has
           ever happened to him，但那是丈夫形容车好、是已经发生的事，并非他在『卖车后要做
           的动作』，把一句描述硬塞成了答案。
               借用原文：the car is the best thing that has ever happened to him
Questions 12 to 15 are based on the conversation you have just heard.

1   W: If you have time right now, I think we need to sit down and discuss what exactly we are
going to do for Jake's education next year. I've been thinking it over, and I'd like to send him to
Westwood Elementary.
W: 如果你现在有时间的话,我觉得我们需要坐下来讨论一下明年Jake的教育问题。我一直在考虑这件事,我
想把他送到Westwood小学。

2   M: You mean that boarding school? I thought we agreed that we would not be sending him away at
such a young age. I admit that their academic record is very strong with many outstanding awards for
achievements in Science and Math, but he is just not mature enough to live on his own at this point.
What about enrolling him in Lakeview School?
M: 你是说那所寄宿学校?我以为我们说好了不会在他这么小的年纪就把他送走。我承认他们的学术成绩非
常优秀,在科学和数学方面获得了许多杰出的奖项,但他现在还不够成熟,不能独自生活。把他送到Lakeview
学校怎么样?

3   W: They do have a great outdoor campus where he could finally learn all those outdoor sports you
have been wanting him to try. With so much greenery and fresh air, I think his current breathing
problems would probably disappear. They did just open, though, so I don't think I feel comfortable
having our son in the first class of a brand-new school.
W: 他们确实有一个很棒的户外校园,在那里他终于可以学习你一直想让他尝试的那些户外运动了。有这么
多的绿化和新鲜空气,我想他目前的呼吸问题可能会消失。不过他们刚开办,所以我不太放心让我们的儿子
成为一所全新学校的第一届学生。

4   M: Yeah, the classrooms would probably smell like paint, making the air in the school all stuffy. What
about our local school then? They have a wonderful arts program that would really challenge Jake in




                        懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/listening/2025-12-2/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 33 -->

terms of creativity and imagination. Being practically next door, one of us could take him to school
every day before going to work.
M: 是啊,教室里可能还有油漆味,让学校里的空气都很闷。那我们本地的学校呢?他们有一个很棒的艺术课
程,能真正在创造力和想象力方面挑战Jake。就在家门口,我们其中一个人每天上班前都可以送他去学校。

5   W: I don't know. They have a reputation of being overly strict with the students, not to mention the
overwhelming amount of homework they assign, which would lead to a lot of pressure for Jake and me.
What if we send him to the place near your mother's house?
W: 我不知道。他们以对学生过于严格而闻名,更不用说布置的作业量大得惊人,这会给Jake和我带来很大压
力。要不我们把他送到你妈妈家附近的那所学校?

12. A) He is not outstanding in Science and Math.
     B) He does not have a strong academic record.
     C) He is not yet mature enough to live on his own.
     D) He does not like Westwood Elementary School.

【题目】 What does the man say about Jake?

【翻译】 男士对Jake有什么评价?
           A) 他在科学和数学方面不突出。                                B) 他没有优秀的学业成绩。

           C) 他还不够成熟,无法独立生活。                                D) 他不喜欢Westwood小学。

【答案】 C （细节题） He is not yet mature enough to live on his own.

【定位】 I admit that their academic record is very strong with many outstanding awards for
           achievements in Science and Math, but he is just not mature enough to live on his own at
           this point.
           我承认他们的学术成绩很强，在科学和数学方面拿过很多杰出的奖项，但他现在还不够成
           熟，没法独立生活。

【信号】 转折 but 之后才是男方真意。这句前半是 I admit…让步夸学校，真正的态度落在 but he is
           just not mature enough to live on his own at this point 之后。听力里 but 之后才是落
           点，前半的赞扬是铺垫，不能当答案。

【替换】 he is just not mature enough to live on his own ↔
           He is not yet mature enough to live on his own.
           几乎原句，just→yet 同义




                         懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/listening/2025-12-2/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 34 -->

【排除】 A) 原词陷阱

          选项说 not outstanding in Science and Math，而录音 outstanding awards for
          achievements in Science and Math 恰恰是说学校在科学数学上很突出。它盗用了原词
          Science and Math，却把"突出"反说成"不突出"。
              借用原文：outstanding awards for achievements in Science and Math
          B) 偷换概念

          录音明说 their academic record is very strong，指的还是学校的学术记录强；选项 He
          does not have a strong academic record. 既把对象从学校换成 Jake，又把"强"反成"不
          强"。
              借用原文：their academic record is very strong
          D) 未提及

          Westwood 是女方想送的 boarding school，男方的顾虑是孩子"不够成熟"，全程没说
          Jake 喜不喜欢这所学校，He does not like Westwood Elementary School. 属凭空添加。


13. A) It is too far away.
   B) It is newly opened.
   C) It lacks outdoor sports facilities.
   D) It has too few first-class teachers.

【题目】 Why does the woman feel uncomfortable about sending Jake to Lakeview School?

【翻译】 女士为什么对把Jake送到Lakeview学校感到不安?
          A) 太远了。            B) 刚开办不久。                  C) 缺少户外运动设施。                            D) 一流教师太少。

【答案】 B （细节题） It is newly opened.

【定位】 They did just open, though, so I don't think I feel comfortable having our son in the first class
          of a brand-new school.
          不过他们才刚开学，所以我不太放心让我们的儿子去一所全新学校的第一届。

【信号】 though 转折 + 不安的直接陈述。前文女方先夸 Lakeview 校园好、空气好，though 一转
          才道出顾虑，紧接 I don't think I feel comfortable having our son in the first class of a
          brand-new school，与题干 feel uncomfortable 直接呼应，是答案句。

【替换】 They did just open ↔ It is newly opened.
          did just open = newly opened
          the first class of a brand-new school ↔ It is newly opened.
          brand-new school 印证"新开"

          It is too far away. 谈的是距离，但女方对 Lakeview 的顾虑是"太新"，文中没说 Lakeview
          远；"近"反而是后面本地学校的优点。距离话题被张冠李戴到了 Lakeview 头上。
              借用原文：Being practically next door
          C) 与原文相反

          录音说 Lakeview a great outdoor campus where he could finally learn all those
          outdoor sports，户外运动是它的优点；选项 It lacks outdoor sports facilities. 把"有大
          操场练户外运动"反说成"缺户外运动设施"。
              借用原文：a great outdoor campus where he could finally learn all those outdoor sports
          D) 未提及

          学，从没提教师数量或质量，属凭空添加。

15. A) Asking Jake which school he himself prefers to enroll in.
   B) Thinking twice about what is best for Jake's education next year.
   C) Consulting her mother about which school Jake is to attend.
   D) Sending Jake to the school near the home of the man's mother.

【题目】 What does the woman suggest at the end of the conversation?

【翻译】 女士在对话结尾建议什么?
          A) 询问Jake本人更愿意就读哪所学校。                                    B) 再三考虑什么对Jake明年的教育最有利。

          C) 咨询她母亲关于Jake该上哪所学校。                                    D) 把Jake送到男士母亲家附近的学校。

【答案】 D （细节题） Sending Jake to the school near the home of the man's mother.

【定位】 What if we send him to the place near your mother's house?
          要不我们把他送到你妈妈家附近的那所学校怎么样？

【信号】 建议句式 What if 在结尾出现。对话最后一句 What if we send him to the place near
          your mother's house? 用 What if 提出新方案，是女方对男方说的，your mother 指男方
          的母亲，正是结尾建议，为答案句。

【替换】 the place near your mother's house ↔
          Sending Jake to the school near the home of the man's mother.
          your mother = the man's mother
          send him to the place ↔ Sending Jake to the school
          send him = sending Jake，place=school

           Consulting her mother about which school Jake is to attend. 盗用结尾的 mother，却

Section C

Directions: In this section, you will hear three passages. At the end of each passage, you will hear three
or four questions. Both the passage and the questions will be spoken only once After you hear a
question, you must choose the best answer from the four choices marked A), B), C) and D). Then mark
the corresponding letter on Answer Sheet 1 with a single line through the centre.

Questions 16 to 18 are based on the passage you have just heard.

1   Analytical decision-making is a decision-making approach where a manager makes important business
decisions only with solid data. This style contrasts with more instinctive leadership styles where
managers make many decisions using instinct or opinion. While analytical decision-makers benefit from a
deliberate, thoughtful approach, this style also has some shortcomings.
分析型决策是一种决策方法,管理者仅凭可靠数据做出重要商业决策。这种风格与更依赖直觉的领导风格形
成对比,后者的管理者运用本能或观点做出许多决策。虽然分析型决策者受益于审慎、深思的方法,但这种
风格也存在一些缺陷。

2   A key flaw with analytical decision-making is that it takes time. While this approach is beneficial
when you have the time and the decision is important, it is problematic when time is crucial. Analytical
decision-makers struggle with deadlines and a sense of urgency with decisions. In situations where you
need more timely action, decisions based on some information and instinct may work better.

分析型决策的一个关键缺陷是耗费时间。虽然当你有时间且决策重要时这种方法是有益的,但当时间紧迫时
就会产生问题。分析型决策者难以应对截止日期和决策的紧迫感。在需要更及时行动的情况下,基于部分信
息和直觉的决策可能效果更好。

3   The desire to be right is a major driver of analytical decision-making. Leaders who use this style don't
want to make mistakes. This commitment, one right way of thinking or doing things, hinders the




                        懒笔记 · https://english-exam.lazynote.cn/cet4/sections/listening/2025-12-2/?f=p · 在线使用效果更好

<!-- source-page: 38 -->

manager's flexibility. Inflexibility causes you to ignore or avoid listening to the thoughts and ideas of
others. You only want to utilize hard data or facts.
追求正确是分析型决策的主要驱动力。采用这种风格的领导者不想犯错。这种对唯一正确思维或做事方式
的执着,妨碍了管理者的灵活性。缺乏灵活性导致你忽视或避免倾听他人的想法和观点。你只想利用硬数据
或事实。

4   Despite its restrictions, analytical decision-making does have several key strengths. By relying on facts
and data, you minimize your potential for a wrong decision. You also get away from the habit of making
guesses or following instincts. Database technology enables business leaders to look to hard evidence
in analyzing target markets and promotional strategies, rather than making assumptions or guesses on
what works.
尽管存在局限性,分析型决策确实具有几个关键优势。通过依赖事实和数据,你将做出错误决策的可能性降
至最低。你也摆脱了猜测或遵循直觉的习惯。数据库技术使商业领导者能够依靠确凿证据来分析目标市场
和促销策略,而不是对有效方法进行假设或猜测。

16. A) It is time consuming.
     B) It is unfit for crucial issues.
     C) It relies too much on solid data.
     D) It dulls a leader's sense of urgency.

【题目】 What does the passage say is a major flaw of analytical decision-making?

【翻译】 文章认为分析式决策的一个主要缺陷是什么?
            A) 它耗费时间。               B) 它不适合关键问题。                            C) 它过于依赖可靠数据。

            D) 它削弱了领导者的紧迫感。

【答案】 A （细节题） It is time consuming.

【定位】 A key flaw with analytical decision-making is that it takes time.
            分析型决策的一个关键缺陷是它很费时间。

【信号】 关键词 key flaw 直接亮出缺点。录音第 2 段开头 A key flaw with analytical decision-
            making is that it takes time 用 A key flaw 这个"明示缺点"信号词领出答案句——耳朵一
            抓到 flaw 就该锁定其后内容，这正是预读时守的信号。

【替换】 it takes time ↔ It is time consuming
            takes time = time consuming 同义改写

<!-- source-page: 39 -->

【排除】 B) 原词陷阱

          录音说的是 it is problematic when time is crucial——"当时间紧迫时它才成问题"，B 却
          把 crucial 搬来说成 It is unfit for crucial issues（不适合关键"问题"），把"时间紧迫"偷换
          成"关键议题"，听到 crucial 就选会中招。
             借用原文：it is problematic when time is crucial
          C) 偷换概念

          It relies too much on solid data 把"靠 solid data"说成缺点，但录音里 makes
          important business decisions only with solid data 是在客观描述这种风格、第 4 段更
          把"靠数据"算作优点，C 把中性/优点歪曲成缺陷，与题干问的 flaw 不符。
             借用原文：makes important business decisions only with solid data
          D) 过度推断

          录音只说分析型决策者 struggle with deadlines and a sense of urgency（应付不来紧迫
          感），并未说它"钝化/削弱"领导的紧迫感；D 的 dulls a leader's sense of urgency 是借
          urgency 一词反向引申，原文无此意。
             借用原文：struggle with deadlines and a sense of urgency
17. A) Depend on digital technology.
   B) Make assumptions and guesses.
   C) Ignore others' thoughts and ideas.
   D) Lay emphasis on thinking flexibly.

【题目】 What does a leader tend to do when they are committed to analytical decision-making?

【翻译】 当领导者致力于分析式决策时,他们往往会做什么?
          A) 依赖数字技术。               B) 做出假设和猜测。                          C) 忽视他人的想法和意见。

          D) 强调灵活思考。

【答案】 C （细节题） Ignore others' thoughts and ideas.

          这种僵化会使你忽视或回避去倾听他人的想法和主意。

【信号】 因果信号 causes 引出结果行为。录音第 3 段 Inflexibility causes you to ignore or avoid
          listening to the thoughts and ideas of others 用因果词 causes 把"僵化"导向具体行
          为"忽视他人想法"——因果信号后面的结果句往往就是"会去做什么"的答案。

          原句压缩成短语，意思不变

18. A) Maximizing the advantage of available resources.
   B) Minimizing the possibility of a wrong decision.

【题目】 What is a key strength of analytical decision-making?

          D) 完善推广策略。

          ——"转好"信号词 strengths 后面那句就是答案句。
